Every grade this world publishes is the worst one and the corporate books are thin. It is a MEASUREMENT and never a rule, and it waits on Part XII's                                                                                 |
| `Corporate Credit H3`    | MISSING | a VERIFY over two claims on ONE issuer that rank differently, and this world's issuers have one line each. It is a measurement and never a rule, and it waits on an issuer having a junior tranche to be junior to                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Corporate Credit H4`    | MISSING | the cash market clears (D2) and the synthetic is §19's CDS book, which exists and has never traded in a scale model (BUILT AND DEAD, `check:existence`). The BASIS between them is one derivation, and nothing computes it                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Corporate Credit H4.a`  | MISSING | the same basis, read the other way: neither leg sets the other. It is refused by construction — a derivative on an uncleared price is forbidden and both books clear on their own — and what is not built is the MEASUREMENT of the gap. Positioned at 21.137 with H4                                                                                                                                   |

## Sovereign

| requirement      | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ---------------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Sovereign A1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` creates a tax due for the named statutory payer off each settled wage and sale leg, and typed public-purchase, transfer and employment agreements create the outlays. Both sides are named parties carrying dated dues rather than a rate on an aggregate                                                                                                                   |
| `Sovereign A1.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` creates a tax due against the named party that remits it, off each settled wage and sale leg and off reported firm income, so no rate is applied to an aggregate. Only three bases exist: nothing taxes payroll separately                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Sovereign A1.b` | PARTIAL | `Funding` creates purchases, transfers and public wages as dues to named payees, so three of the four kinds reach somebody. Interest is not among them: no sovereign coupon is an outlay the programme plans for                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Sovereign A1.c` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `Programme::to_raise` subtracts money actually held from what fell due and what was committed, and the money held is whatever remitted tax settled into the account. The gap is a leftover of two real flows, and nothing states a deficit to hit or reads one as an input                                                                                                           |
| `Sovereign A2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `Programme::to_raise` sizes the need as redemptions plus outlays less the buffer, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` sizes its gap off the treasury`s own dated schedules and raises it through packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `must_raise` before the books open, so the issue is brought ahead of the money leaving                                     |
| `Sovereign A2.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `Programme` counts redemptions falling due beside what the treasury has committed to pay, so the need it raises against is both together and never the deficit alone                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign A2.b` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` sizes the week's issue from schedules that already exist and posts it before the outlays settle, so the plan is made against what is coming rather than against an empty account                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign A2.c` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads a tenor from `params` and issues at it, so a maturity is chosen. It is one declared tenor rather than a mix, and nothing weighs a cheap short issue that rolls against a dear long one that does not                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Sovereign A3`   | MET     | the treasury holds one money account like any other party and packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs pays every outlay out of it, recording `ShortOfMoney` rather than overdrawing it. There is no facility that clears a negative balance                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Sovereign A3.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs refuses a payment the balance cannot cover with `ShortOfMoney` and records the refusal, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `handle` takes the issuer to its buffer, a deferred outlay or back to the market. An empty account is an event with somewhere to go                                                                                                                     |
| `Sovereign A3.b` | MET     | nothing advances money to a treasury. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_funding.rs opens a central-bank facility to a bank and to nobody else, and a short treasury resolves through packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `handle` as buffer, then deferred outlay, then a return to the market. The absence is unguarded: no check refuses an advance, because no mechanism could make one                          |
| `Sovereign A4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `default_cause` makes non-payment in the sovereign`s own money a REFUSAL rather than an inability, and willingness is durable party-owned mandate state a decision moves. Default is therefore a choice and the choice is recorded                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Sovereign A4.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs writes willingness through `now_stands` as durable state owned by that sovereign, and a decision moves it. It is a variable one party holds rather than a constant the model asserts                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign A4.b` | PARTIAL | `default_cause` makes non-payment in the issuer's own money a refusal and non-payment in a money it cannot create an inability, which is the distinction the clause calls the whole of sovereign credit risk. Nothing exercises it: a treasury owes only its own region's money                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Sovereign B1`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `SovereignPaper::coupon` answers `None` for a bill and a rate for a bond, and each produces its own schedule — a zero-coupon line falls due in principal alone, and packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `accrued_per_unit` accretes its discount rather than paying it a coupon. They are still one `Class::Claim` distinguished by a field, which is the flag B1 says they must not be |
| `Sovereign B2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `default_cause` takes the issuer`s home currency and the currency the due is owed in, and that single comparison is what decides refusal from inability — the difference A4.b calls the whole of sovereign credit risk                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Sovereign B3`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs keys a holding by (holder, instrument), so every holder of one line is fungible in it and the issued amount is one number. A re-opening that adds to an existing line rather than creating a new one is not built: each week`s funding brings a fresh line                                                                                                                                       |
| `Sovereign B3.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `issue` allocates a new id every time and has no door that adds to an existing line, so every tap would be a separate instrument. Nothing re-opens anything, and the line and the issue are therefore the same object                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Sovereign B4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs carries no seniority on sovereign paper, so the ranking cannot vary, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` pays every holder of a line per unit of par out of one schedule. Pari passu is the construction rather than a rule laid over it                                                                                                                    |
| `Sovereign B5`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs reads a sovereign default only off a schedule whose state is `Failed` — a missed payment and nothing else. No covenant is struck on sovereign paper and no acceleration exists to trigger                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign B6`   | PARTIAL | no sovereign line carries a call, a make-whole or a non-call period, so there is no early-termination regime to exercise. The buyback and switch the issuer manages its curve with instead is `CurveOperation`, which has no caller                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign B7`   | MET     | a sovereign default opens a `SOVEREIGN_EXCHANGE` process in packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs and never an estate, so no holder acquires a claim on anything seizable. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/mortality.rs consumes a sovereign`s final payment failure without realising assets                                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign C1`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `AuctionAnnouncement` carries the line and the size an auction is announced in, and nothing constructs one. The treasury brings its paper and posts in the same week, so no bidder ever sees a calendar ahead of the book                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign C1.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `AuctionAnnouncement` carries the line and the size an auction would be announced in, and nothing constructs one. No bidder sees a calendar, because none is published                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Sovereign C1.b` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `TreasuryIssues` posts the shortfall packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `must_raise` computed from its own gap, so the size is the issuer's own and comes out of its programme. It is not announced ahead of the book, so nobody can prepare against it                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign C2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `clear` returns one price every fill settles at, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs declares its auction `BuyersCompete`, so every winning bidder pays the stop-out. There is one solver and no second answer to the same bids                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Sovereign C3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `PrimaryDealers` is registered on the sovereign row and posts for every dealer into the book the treasury auctions in. It bids at its own `price_outlook` and for what `obliged_size` leaves it, so the obligation is real and what it costs is its own                                                                                                                              |
| `Sovereign C3.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `obliged_size` answers nothing for a desk at its position limit or out of cash, and packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs answers `NoDemand` where nobody bid — so an auction fails exactly when the dealers step back, and no central bank stands behind it. What the privilege IS in exchange is not modelled, so the bargain is one-sided                                            |
| `Sovereign C3.b` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `PrimaryDealers` names its own reservation and takes the fill at whatever the book clears at, onto a position it carries at `Carrying::Market` — so a bid that was too high is marked against it and lands on its own account                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Sovereign C4`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs keeps every auction`s requested units, filled units and proceeds as a durable session, so the tail and the cover ratio are readable from it. Nothing reads them: neither statistic is computed or published                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Sovereign C5`   | PARTIAL | weak demand resolves as a worse price, because the treasury posts a size and the book clears where the bids reach. The other resolution is not built: `sovereign.rs handle` can return `ComeBackToTheMarket` at a different size or maturity and nothing acts on it                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign C6`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs `run_book` settles an auction fill as an ordinary delivery-versus-payment instruction, so the proceeds land in the account of the treasury in the same pass the paper leaves it. A fill that cannot pay is a recorded failure and not a credited balance                                                                                                                                          |
| `Sovereign C7`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs reads `auction_of` for every session of the week and journals the issuer, the line, the ask, the fill and the proceeds wherever the fill fell short of the ask. Unsold paper stays with the issuer and the account is lower than the programme assumed                                                                                                                               |
| `Sovereign D1`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/systems.rs declares one book per sovereign line and packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs prints a price per unit where it crosses. No participant posts into a sovereign line after issue, so the secondary market has a venue and no parties                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Sovereign D2`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `yield_to` derives a return from its price and only this way round, on the line's own `Convention`. No sovereign line's yield is published, so nothing downstream can invert one either                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Sovereign D3`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/benchmarks.rs `curve_at` fits a tenor through observed points and is the one owner of the fit. It fits over points a caller hands it, and no caller builds those points from sovereign prints, so the curve is a function with no observations                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Sovereign D3.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/benchmarks.rs `curve_at` is the only fit in the tree and no other module derives a tenor point, so the curve has one owner and no second answer exists to disagree with it                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Sovereign D3.b` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/benchmarks.rs `CurvePoint` carries a `CurveProvenance`, so a point says whether it was traded, interpolated, extrapolated or never traded, and `curve_at` never reads its own previous output. A consumer that needs a real price is not told: nothing reads the provenance before using a point                                                                                                  |
| `Sovereign D3.c` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/calendar.rs `Convention` is the one place a compounding basis is stated and `year_fraction` the one function that applies it, so two conventions cannot answer one question. The curve does not publish which it used, so a consumer takes the convention rather than being told it                                                                                                                          |
| `Sovereign D4`   | MISSING | nothing prices as a spread to the sovereign curve. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/benchmarks.rs selects the lines whose issuer is a treasury and fits a curve; no lender, holder or issuer reads the result, so the benchmark benchmarks nothing                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign D5`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs can encumber units through a lien and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `lends_against` is the haircut a lender applies, refusing collateral already pledged. No repo pledges sovereign paper, so neither is exercised on a sovereign line                                                                                                                                       |
| `Sovereign D6`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `bid_offer` reads a width off the two best posted levels rather than applying a prior, and has no caller. A primary dealer posts a bid and the treasury an offer, so a width exists in the book; nothing measures it                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign E1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs `of_instrument` lists a line by its holders and `of_holder` lists a holder by its lines, so who holds how much of which sovereign line is one walk over one store. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs takes exactly that walk to read the holdouts on an open exchange                                                                                                               |
| `Sovereign E1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs is the one writer of a holding and indexes a line by its holders as well as a holder by its lines, so `of_instrument` answers the question in a single walk. Sovereign paper is a holding like any other and sits in no second store                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Sovereign E2`   | MISSING | the clause asks for six holder classes buying for six different reasons, which is what gives an auction two sides. None posts: no bank buys a liquidity buffer, no insurer buys duration, no central bank buys as policy, no foreign official buys reserves, no fund takes relative value and no household buys directly                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign E2.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_capital.rs weights an asset by its issuer's grade, which is the reason a bank would hold sovereign paper. No bank holds any, so the buffer motive reaches no book                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign E2.b` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/insurers.rs `InsurerMatching` bids from a price outlook rather than from a liability it must match, and no insurer holds a sovereign line. Duration is nobody's reason for anything                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Sovereign E2.c` | MISSING | no central bank holds sovereign paper, because packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `OpenMarketPurchase` is constructed by nothing. Policy is nobody's reason to hold a line                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Sovereign E2.d` | MISSING | no foreign official holder exists, because the world has one country and one currency. Nothing holds a line as a reserve                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign E2.e` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/funds.rs `FundMandates` carries what a fund may hold and no fund holds a sovereign line, so relative value is nobody's reason either                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Sovereign E2.f` | MISSING | no household or firm holds a bill directly, so no saver substitutes between a fund and the paper that fund would have bought. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs `prefers_paper` is that choice and no production code names it                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Sovereign E3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `worth` is units times the latest cleared print for every holding, sovereign paper included, and returns missing rather than a basis where no market has printed. A holder`s sovereign position is marked the same way as anything else it owns                                                                                                                                               |
| `Sovereign E4`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Leg::Pledge` encumbers named units and packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs refuses to move what is pledged, so paper CAN be pledged and cannot be pledged twice. Nothing pledges a sovereign line, so the haircut packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `lends_against` would apply is never applied to one                                                                            |
| `Sovereign E5`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_capital.rs weights an asset from the issuer`s grade through `classified_weight`, and a sovereign issuer is one `FailureMode::Never` cannot reach — so it weights lightest. It is not a declared zero, and no bank`s holding of sovereign paper is what puts it there: no bank holds any                                                                                                      |
| `Sovereign F1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` pays a due to every holder of record per unit of par, read off the register at the week's open, and pays the issuer's own holding nothing. What the coupon has earned in between is packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `accrued_per_unit`, taken every week at b1 and by the book that prices it dirty                                                                       |
| `Sovereign F2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `accrued_per_unit` accretes a line with no coupon against `first_of_line` — the price that line itself first cleared at — over the life its own principal is owed for, and never off a curve. packages/kernel-rs/src/assembly.rs `accrues` takes that read for every line every week at b1                                                                                                         |
| `Sovereign F3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `schedule_of` gives every dated line its principal payment from the contract, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` pays it out of the account of the treasury at maturity. The redeemed holding dies in that same instruction, so outstanding issuance falls through the one writer of the register                                                                   |
| `Sovereign F4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `Programme` counts redemptions falling due in the need it must raise, so the issue that funds a maturity is brought into the same book as any other and takes whatever the market charges that week                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Sovereign F5`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `CurveOperation` carries the line bought in, the line switched into and what the operation costs, and nothing constructs one. The issuer never manages its own curve                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Sovereign G1`   | MISSING | the VERIFY asks that a sovereign short in its own money inflates rather than defaults. Neither half is reachable: there is no central bank to create the money and no price level a creation would move                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Sovereign G2`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `default_cause` separates the two: an issuer that is willing but owes money it cannot create fails by inability, and every other non-payment is refusal. The foreign branch is unreachable, because a treasury issues and owes only the money of its own region, so no sovereign is ever in the position the clause is about                                                         |
| `Sovereign G3`   | PARTIAL | a failed due opens a `SOVEREIGN_EXCHANGE` process on the LINE that failed rather than on the issuer, so a default is selective, and no estate opens because a treasury reaches none of the three doors in packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/mortality.rs that create one. The negotiation is absent: the process opens, restates its holdout face each week, and nothing offers terms or closes it                                  |
| `Sovereign G4`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `holdout_face` sums the face standing outside the hands of the issuer for every open exchange, and the mechanism journals it each week. There is no offer to hold out from: no replacement line is proposed, no holder tenders, and no paper is exchanged                                                                                                                            |
| `Sovereign G5`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `TreasuryIssues` posts no market at all once a default event stands against the issuer, so the consequence is exclusion, and nothing liquidates it because no estate opens. The rating half is absent: packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/ratings.rs grades a party off published accounts and no treasury publishes any, so a sovereign grade has neither producer nor consumer       |
| `Sovereign H1`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `OpenMarketPurchase` carries the buyer, the seller, the line, the face and the price, and nothing constructs one. No central bank posts in any book, so no policy purchase is ever sized or made                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Sovereign H2`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `reserve_creation` values a purchase and refuses one whose seller is the issuer, and it is unreached. With no purchase there is no reserve created and the base does not move                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Sovereign H3`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `central_bank_remittance` is unreached. No central bank holds sovereign paper, so no coupon accrues on its book and nothing returns to the treasury                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Sovereign H3.a` | MISSING | no central bank holds a sovereign line, so nothing accrues on its book and no receivable stands on its sheet. packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `accrued` is the read that would produce the figure and no holder calls it                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Sovereign H4`   | PARTIAL | the forbidden side of the boundary holds, because a treasury raises before it spends and no advance reaches it. The permitted side does not exist: there are no open-market operations, so nothing is being drawn between and the policy choice the clause describes is not yet a choice anybody can make                                                                                                                           |
| `Sovereign H5`   | MISSING | the VERIFY compares two readings of one debt and there is nothing to read. No central bank holds a sovereign line, so neither the consolidated statement nor the accounting one has a subject                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Sovereign I1`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `BondFuture` names a deliverable line, a contract count, face per contract, a delivery week and the margin posted, and `future_settlement` marks it to the cash price of that bond. Nothing constructs one and no market in a future is declared                                                                                                                                     |
| `Sovereign I1.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `net_basis` is the future price less the financed cash position, and it is unreached. There is no future price to measure against and nothing finances a sovereign line                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Sovereign I2`   | MISSING | the clause names three reasons to be on one side or the other and no participant posts a bond future at all. A duration mandate short of duration, a holder over its sovereign target and a dealer quoting both ways are absent from that book because the book does not exist                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Sovereign I3`   | MISSING | nothing goes long a cash sovereign line against a short future, because neither leg exists: no future is constructed, and `Leg::Pledge` never encumbers sovereign paper to finance one. The repo demand the clause calls the largest real source of it is not in the model                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Sovereign I3.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `basis_trade_allowed` refuses a position whose posted margin does not cover the drawdown, and it is unreached. The FORBID holds only because no basis trader exists to lose anything                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

## Short-Term Debt

| requirement            | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ---------------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Short-Term Debt A1`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` brings paper with a tenor and a coupon read from `params`. A1.a`s discount instrument does not exist: every line it brings carries a coupon                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Short-Term Debt A1.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs `Paper` carries a face, a price and two dates and no coupon at all, so the discount is the whole return by construction. Nothing accretes it over the life, so the return arrives entirely in the week the paper repays                                                                                     |
| `Short-Term Debt A1.b` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs reads its tenor in weeks from `params`, and the shape that would make a piece of paper short because its maturity is near — `Paper`, whose `term` is the distance between the week it was issued and the week it matures — is reached by nothing                                                            |
| `Short-Term Debt A1.c` | MISSING | no instrument states where its holder ranks, so short-term paper cannot rank with an issuer's other senior debt. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/estate.rs `Rank` orders claims in a wind-up and no paper carries the rank it would be given                                                                                                                   |
| `Short-Term Debt A1.d` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `issue` takes no early-termination term and no field holds one, so a bill has no option in it. The clause asks for none and the type provides none                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Short-Term Debt A2`   | PARTIAL | the price half holds: packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs `Brings` brings discount paper with no coupon into a declared book and packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs prints where it crosses. The yield half does not: `Paper::yield_on` derives the rate from the cleared price and the term, in that direction, and no production code names it |
| `Short-Term Debt A2.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs `Paper::yield_on` takes a `Convention` and hands it to `yield_to`, so a day count would be part of the answer, and no production code names it. Nothing derives a yield on short paper at all, so there is no number for a convention to be a material part of                                              |
| `Short-Term Debt A3`   | PARTIAL | the short borrower is the issuer and discount paper is priced by its book rather than a shared coupon; issuer-specific buyer limits remain incomplete                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Short-Term Debt B1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads what falls due inside its own window and brings paper for exactly that shortfall                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Short-Term Debt B2`   | MISSING | `short_term_debt` runs its own `Brings` (a PLACEHOLDER, like `corporate_credit`'s, reading a borrower's receipts as nothing) — and `mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs Limit`, `Rolled`, `roll`, `wall`, `prefers_paper`, `spread_over_bill` and `redeem` have no caller. No participant posts in a paper book                                                        |
| `Short-Term Debt B3`   | MISSING | `short_term_debt` runs its own `Brings` (a PLACEHOLDER, like `corporate_credit`'s, reading a borrower's receipts as nothing) — and `mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs Limit`, `Rolled`, `roll`, `wall`, `prefers_paper`, `spread_over_bill` and `redeem` have no caller. | `Short-Term Debt B3.a` | MISSING | nothing rolls. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/short_term_debt.rs `Brings` journals an intention to issue and proposes no instruction, so no maturing paper is replaced by a new issue a market could decline                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Short-Term Debt B3.b` | MISSING | no run is possible because no roll is attempted. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `handle` is the only place an issuer short of cash goes looking, and no corporate or bank issuer reaches it                                                                                                                                                      |

## Equity

| requirement   | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Equity A1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs (a share is the residual, ranking below all debt — packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/estate.rs `Rank::Equity` is last)                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Equity A1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/estate.rs `Rank::Equity` is the last rank in the waterfall, so a shareholder is paid only once every debt claim has been                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Equity A1.b` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs `residual` answers with two figures, what is left to shareholders and what is short to creditors, so a shortfall cannot become a claim on a holder. Nothing asks it: the FORBID holds because no residual is ever taken, not because the answer is used                                        |
| `Equity A2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `Class::Share` counted in its own unit, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs `Line.outstanding` changed only by `apply`                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Equity A2.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs `Line::outstanding` moves only through `apply`, which takes a `ShareEvent` — issued, bought back, split or cancelled — so no path changes a count without naming what happened                                                                                                                 |
| `Equity A3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `ccy_of` (a share is quoted in its issuer`s money)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Equity A4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs (a share is brought with `matures: None`, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs says why there is no field to leave unset)                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Equity A5`   | MISSING | the `equity` system runs `mechanisms/equity.rs Floating`, which brings a share line and journals; `mechanisms/equity.rs` is imported by nothing. No participant posts in an equity book, so no share price ever clears and nothing in §10 B–G happens                                                                                      |
| `Equity A5.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs `control_needs` is what a majority would cost in shares and no production code names it. No participant posts in an equity book, so no stake is ever accumulated towards one                                                                                                                   |
| `Equity A5.b` | MISSING | the `equity` system runs `mechanisms/equity.rs Floating`, which brings a share line and journals; `mechanisms/equity.rs` is imported by nothing. No participant posts in an equity book, so no share price ever clears and nothing in §10 B–G happens                                                                                      |
| `Equity A6`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `display` names a share by its issuer. Its one caller, `observer.rs display_name`, is dead code                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Equity B1`   | MISSING | the `equity` system runs `mechanisms/equity.rs Floating`, which brings a share line and journals; `mechanisms/equity.rs` is imported by nothing. No participant posts in an equity book, so no share price ever clears and nothing in §10 B–G happens                                                                                      |
| `Equity B2`   | MISSING | the `equity` system runs `mechanisms/equity.rs Floating`, which brings a share line and journals; `mechanisms/equity.rs` is imported by nothing. ## Money Market

| requirement         | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------- | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Money Market A1`   | MISSING | `Interbank` reads each bank's reserve position at d6, after the week's flows have left it where they left it, and a bank short of its buffer BRINGS a week's paper on its own name with a book of its own — so one borrower's paper prices differently from another's and a name the market doubts finds no bid. `MoneyMarketBanks` lends into those books at its own view of that name, valuing paper it has no history of from the issuer's other paper or from the contract itself, and it SELLS its own paper in its own auction at a reserve: `reserve` turns the most it will pay — its own cost of funds plus what the standing facility would add — into the least it will take for a bill that repays par, so a borrower that would rather draw the facility does not sell. Repo stands beside it: `can_pledge` finds the borrower's largest unencumbered position in somebody else's claim, the paper carries that pledge as a TERM, packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs encumbers that much of it in the same instruction as the money, and a lender prices secured paper on `lends_against` — the collateral, the tenor and its own view of that issuer's credit — rather than on the borrower's name. A term issue stands beside the week: `terms_out` locks the funding in where the bank's own outlook of what credit costs is above what its own money costs it now, so two banks with different histories bring different tenors and each gets its own book |
| `Money Market A1.a` | MISSING | no bank lends reserves to another, so nothing is redistributed. packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `MoneyIsConserved` would see such a movement leave the total unchanged, and no interbank loan is ever proposed for it to see                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Money Market A1.b` | MISSING | nothing pairs one bank's deficit with another's surplus. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `MoneyMarketBanks` posts a schedule and no book is declared for it, so the two sides never meet                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Money Market A2`   | MISSING | `Interbank` reads each bank's reserve position at d6, after the week's flows have left it where they left it, and a bank short of its buffer BRINGS a week's paper on its own name with a book of its own — so one borrower's paper prices differently from another's and a name the market doubts finds no bid. `MoneyMarketBanks` lends into those books at its own view of that name, valuing paper it has no history of from the issuer's other paper or from the contract itself, and it SELLS its own paper in its own auction at a reserve: `reserve` turns the most it will pay — its own cost of funds plus what the standing facility would add — into the least it will take for a bill that repays par, so a borrower that would rather draw the facility does not sell. A term issue stands beside the week: `terms_out` locks the funding in where the bank's own outlook of what credit costs is above what its own money costs it now, so two banks with different histories bring different tenors and each gets its own book |
| `Money Market A2.a` | MISSING | no bank derives a buffer from how liquid its own funding is. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_funding.rs reads a declared ratio from `params`, which is the floor the clause says may sit under a preference rather than the preference itself                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Money Market A2.b` | MISSING | `Interbank` reads each bank's reserve position at d6, after the week's flows have left it where they left it, and a bank short of its buffer BRINGS a week's paper on its own name with a book of its own — so one borrower's paper prices differently from another's and a name the market doubts finds no bid. `MoneyMarketBanks` lends into those books at its own view of that name, valuing paper it has no history of from the issuer's other paper or from the contract itself, and it SELLS its own paper in its own auction at a reserve: `reserve` turns the most it will pay — its own cost of funds plus what the standing facility would add — into the least it will take for a bill that repays par, so a borrower that would rather draw the facility does not sell. Repo stands beside it: `can_pledge` finds the borrower's largest unencumbered position in somebody else's claim, the paper carries that pledge as a TERM, packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs encumbers that much of it in the same instruction as the money, and a lender prices secured paper on `lends_against` — the collateral, the tenor and its own view of that issuer's credit — rather than on the borrower's name. A term issue stands beside the week: `terms_out` locks the funding in where the bank's own outlook of what credit costs is above what its own money costs it now, so two banks with different histories bring different tenors and each gets its own book |
| `Money Market A3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `Interbank` runs at d6 — after the week's work and before any book opens — and reads what the flows actually left in each bank's reserve account rather than an opening balance. A bank short of its buffer brings paper there and nowhere earlier                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Money Market A3.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/world.rs `Phases` orders d6 before e2 and f, so the paper a short bank brings is in a book before anybody posts to it and the session clears after both. Nothing sizes a shortfall against an opening balance and settles the difference at the close                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Money Market B1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `MoneyMarketBanks::orders` posts out of this bank's OWN position and its OWN cost of funds on both sides: it sells its own paper at `reserve` — its deposit rate plus what the facility would add — and buys another name's at what that name is worth to it. Neither side is assigned, so who lends and who borrows is the outcome of two banks funded differently naming different levels                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

| `Money Market B3.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `can_pledge` takes only a claim somebody else issued, so a share, a good and the borrower's own paper are ineligible, and `lends_into` refuses collateral its issuer also issued the paper. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_funding.rs applies the same test for a central-bank draw                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Money Market B3.b` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `lends_against` divides the collateral's value by the tenor AND by this lender's own view of that ISSUER's credit, read from its standing terms — so the same paper from a doubted name raises less, and a lender with no view of the issuer lends nothing rather than one haircut per type                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

| `Money Market B6`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `Interbank` brings a week's paper or a term's, and each issue is its own book — the week because a week is this world's atom, the term because `money_market.term` says how long one is. There is no overnight tenor to have a book                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Money Market B6.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/money_market.rs `terms_out` puts both tenors in the market out of the borrowers' own outlooks, and `term_spread` reads the difference of two rates that cleared rather than a number anybody set. Nothing calls it yet, because no book has printed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Money Market B7`   | MET     | a name cannot fund because there is no market to fund in. The clause holds by absence, which is not the same as being representable                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Money Market C1`   | MISSING | `Interbank` reads each bank's reserve position at d6, after the week's flows have left it where they left it, and a bank short of its buffer BRINGS a week's paper on its own name with a book of its own — so one borrower's paper prices differently from another's and a name the market doubts finds no bid. `MoneyMarketBanks` lends into those books at its own view of that name, valuing paper it has no history of from the issuer's other paper or from the contract itself, and it SELLS its own paper in its own auction at a reserve: `reserve` turns the most it will pay — its own cost of funds plus what the standing facility would add — into the least it will take for a bill that repays par, so a borrower that would rather draw the facility does not sell. Repo stands beside it: `can_pledge` finds the borrower's largest unencumbered position in somebody else's claim, the paper carries that pledge as a TERM, packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs encumbers that much of it in the same instruction as the money, and a lender prices secured paper on `lends_against` — the collateral, the tenor and its own view of that issuer's credit — rather than on the borrower's name. A term issue stands beside the week: `terms_out` locks the funding in where the bank's own outlook of what credit costs is above what its own money costs it now, so two banks with different histories bring different tenors and each gets its own book |
| `Money Market C1.a` | MISSING | no deposit facility exists, so no cash is parked at a central bank and nothing leaves the banking system that way. The only movement between a bank and the central bank is the collateralised advance, which runs the other way                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Money Market C2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_funding.rs `BankFunding` opens a typed, dated central-bank facility contract and settles newly issued reserves to the borrowing bank                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Money Market C3`   | MISSING | `Interbank` reads each bank's reserve position at d6, after the week's flows have left it where they left it, and a bank short of its buffer BRINGS a week's paper on its own name with a book of its own — so one borrower's paper prices differently from another's and a name the market doubts finds no bid. `MoneyMarketBanks` lends into those books at its own view of that name, valuing paper it has no history of from the issuer's other paper or from the contract itself, and it SELLS its own paper in its own auction at a reserve: `reserve` turns the most it will pay — its own cost of funds plus what the standing facility would add — into the least it will take for a bill that repays par, so a borrower that would rather draw the facility does not sell.
