## Treasury

| requirement     | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Treasury A1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs `kinds::TREASURY` with an account it pays out of, and packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs refuses a payment it cannot make, so A1.a`s balance can run low                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Treasury A1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs pays every treasury outlay out of the balance it holds and refuses one it cannot cover with `ShortOfMoney`, so the balance is real and running low is a recorded refusal rather than a negative number                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Treasury A2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `currency_of(region)` read through the country                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Treasury A3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs `of_holder` is the cash and the instruments a treasury owns and packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `of_issuer` is every line it issued, so the balance sheet is a read of two indexes. No equity figure is stored anywhere, which is what keeps A3.a a read                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Treasury A3.a` | MET     | no equity figure is stored for a treasury anywhere, so what it is worth is `of_holder` less what `of_issuer` says it owes, computed when somebody asks. Nothing refuses the answer for being negative                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Treasury B1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` turns each live public-purchase, public-transfer and treasury-employment agreement into a scheduled payment to its named producer, beneficiary or worker                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Treasury B2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs schedules each issued line’s coupon on that line, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` reads current holders and pays each holder pro rata                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Treasury B3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads separately decided purchase and transfer agreements rather than a constant outlay, and its public payroll is the engagements packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs pays — the treasury hires down the same path as anybody else. Its named-payer tax bases move with settled wages, sales and reported profit                                                                                                           |
| `Treasury B3.a` | MISSING | no outlay moves with the cycle, because nothing measures one and no transfer responds to a household losing work. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs reads a declared per-head transfer from `params`, so outlays are a constant and the constraint never tightens from that side                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Treasury B4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` distributes principal due on a maturing line across every current holder and retires each delivered holding                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Treasury C1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` levies income and payroll tax on each settled wage, consumption tax on each settled sale, and profit tax on each named firm’s own lagged account statement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Treasury C1.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` creates a tax due against each named payer and ordinary settlement debits that payer's own account, so the flow is real at both ends. The base for the corporate tax is the income packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs reported, which is the payer's own statement; the wage and sale bases are read off settled legs rather than off anything the payer stated                                                                 |
| `Treasury C2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` derives a tax due from settled money legs and from reported firm income, so receipts move with the bases that produced them and fall when those bases fall                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Treasury C3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs creates one tax schedule for each named statutory payer, and `TreasuryIssues` reads receipts only by summing schedules owed to that treasury                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Treasury D1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads outgoing and incoming schedules plus cash and the durable buffer before bringing paper; `TreasuryIssues` repeats that live read when it sizes the auction order                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Treasury D2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `TreasuryIssues` posts a sell into a book that must clear, and packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs settles what crosses                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Treasury D2.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `TreasuryIssues` posts a size and a maturity and no level of its own where the venue lets the buyers compete, so the issuer chooses what and how much and packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs decides at what price                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Treasury D3`   | MET     | nothing advances money to a treasury. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs sizes the gap through packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `must_raise`, from what the account actually holds, packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs refuses an outlay the balance cannot cover with `ShortOfMoney`, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_funding.rs `facility_borrower` admits only a bank — so a shortfall stays a shortfall and no facility creates a claim of a central bank on a treasury |
| `Treasury D3.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `OpenMarketPurchase` carries the buyer, the seller, the line, the face and the price, and `reserve_creation` refuses one whose seller is the issuer — which is exactly the line between a policy purchase and an overdraft. Nothing constructs either, so the permitted act never happens                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Treasury D4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads every payment falling in its forward window against incoming schedules, current cash and the treasury’s durable buffer mandate before it brings dated paper                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Treasury D4.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `maturing_by` is the maturity profile — what falls due by a week, read off the lines themselves — and no production code names it. Every issue carries one declared tenor, so the profile is a single wall by construction, and nothing is pre-funded                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Treasury D4.b` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `must_raise` subtracts the mandated buffer from what the treasury holds before sizing an issue, so the buffer is money kept back rather than a line in a plan, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `handle` draws on it first when an auction falls short                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Treasury D5`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs `run_book` settles only crossed quantity, so unsold paper stays with the issuer, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `handle` takes what is still short to the buffer, then to a deferred outlay, then back to the market, in that order                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Treasury D5.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `clear` obliges nobody: it crosses the levels that were posted and leaves the rest unfilled. An order naming no level is admitted only as auction supply under `BuyersCompete`, so no bid can be the residual buyer of a remainder                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Treasury D6`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `issued_of` is what a line has outstanding and packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs `held_total` is what is held of it, and issuance and redemption are the only writers of either. The ownership audit reads both and reconciles them                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Treasury E1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` chooses the declared tenor for each newly dated issue and the resulting maturity profile remains on the issued lines rather than in a debt-total field                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Treasury E2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` runs the programme every week and sizes that auction from packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `must_raise` against live cash and live schedules, taking its maturity from the declared tenor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Treasury E3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs `write` retains each auction print with its provenance and packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `schedule_of` retains the dated cash flows the issue promised. The cost of the debt is therefore derived from what was transacted, and no party sets it                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Treasury E4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `TreasuryIssues` puts the whole live shortfall into the quantity of its sell order, so a heavier size meets unchanged bid schedules in the same call book and moves the print it clears at                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Treasury F1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` creates purchases and transfers as bilateral dues and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` creates its payroll the same way, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` settles them as money received by the named counterparty                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Treasury F2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` creates tax as a bilateral obligation of each named payer, and packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs `money_delta` debits the money that payer itself holds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Treasury F3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `schedule_of` puts the interest on the sovereign line, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` reads `of_instrument` on the payment date and delivers to every current holder                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Treasury F4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Instruction` carries every fiscal payment as one atomic money leg between a treasury and a private counterparty, and the flows audit reads those same settled legs. The fiscal balance and private net saving are two views of one transaction rather than an enforced identity                                                                                                                                                                               |

## Firm

| requirement              | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------ | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Firm A1`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs and packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs (a firm is a named party with an account and rows)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Firm A2`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs puts a firm in a region and packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs reads its money through the region's country. Its sector is not a label beside it but the plant it holds: packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `made_with` says what that plant makes, which is what the firm sells, and the `Way` it runs says what it buys. A sector code kept beside the plant would be a second representation of the same fact, and it would be the one nothing read                                                                                                                  |
| `Firm A3`                | MET     | firms differ in all three. Size is the plant each holds, seeded with a real spread in units and in basis rather than the same three units for everybody; cost is each firm's own `picks` over its own input basis and its own wage bill, so two firms facing different prices run different ways; and leverage is what each has issued and owes in packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs, which is what `booked_equity` in packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs reads a residual out of. The dispersion is what gives the books two sides                                                                   |
| `Firm A4`                | MET     | only a FIRM issues a `Class::Share` line now — a share is the issuer for equity, and one a household issued is a claim on nobody's residual. packages/kernel-rs/src/bin/world_runs.rs gives every such line named owners who are not the issuer, so the residual has holders, and packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `booked_equity` is what that claim is ON: what the firm holds less what it owes                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Firm B1`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `Reporting` takes revenue from settlement's own record of each realised sale — what the units fetched, in a trade whose other side is a named buyer — so revenue is quantity sold times price achieved and nothing else. It is never a growth rate applied to last week; how many books cross in this world is a measurement for later, not a property of the mechanism                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Firm B1.a`              | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs prints a firm's revenue only where its goods book crossed, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `GoodsSellers` posts from that firm's own cost. No growth rate is applied to a previous period anywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Firm B1.b`              | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `Reporting` publishes each week's revenue against the revenue it published the week before, read off its own previous publication — so the baseline a firm is compared with is its own last measured week, and there is no compounded path anywhere for it to be compared with instead                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Firm B2`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` draws the recipe`s inputs as `Leg::Destroy` legs at their own lot cost                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Firm B3`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` pays named households, and `Making` reads the same engagements into the batch cost                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Firm B4`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `OperatingFlows::cash` is `operating_profit(revenue, what it bought, what it paid for labour)` and every part is read off settled legs, so the result is a residual and nothing stops it being negative. `Reporting` publishes it beside what it was made of                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Firm B4.a`              | MET     | no cost line is struck as a share of revenue anywhere — every part of packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `OperatingFlows` is a settled leg — so no margin is an attractor and none was fitted to. `Reporting` publishes `margin` as operating profit over what it actually sold, and a week with no sales has no margin rather than one of nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Firm B4.b`              | MET     | every part of the cost comes from a `Leg::Money` this firm PAID, so each has a named payee by construction — packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs refuses a one-sided leg. There is no cost line struck as a share of revenue and none without somebody on the other end of it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Firm B5`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `OperatingFlows` keeps what the units it sold had cost it apart from what it paid for labour and what it paid out and got no units back for, and `fixed` is the second and third together. Only the first moves with what it sold, so its margin moves more than its revenue and the reason is the structure — `Reporting` says the fixed part beside the total, so a reader can see it rather than infer it                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Firm B6`                | MET     | a cost is booked from a `Leg::Money` the firm paid and packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs refuses a leg with one side, so what a firm books as cost and what its payee received are the same row read twice. There is no second tally to reconcile against, which is what having one representation means                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Firm C1`                | MET     | what a firm holds is its rows in packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs and nothing else — its account, its stock, its plant — each at what packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `carrying_value` says its own declared treatment carries it at. There is no second list of assets to disagree with the register                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Firm C2`                | MET     | what a firm owes is what it issued that others hold, which packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `booked_equity` reads off packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs directly, plus the unpaid dues against it in packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs. Both are actual rows with named counterparties; neither is a figure kept beside them                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Firm C3`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `equity` is a read of assets minus liabilities and can be negative, and what it reads is each position under the treatment its own holder declared                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Firm C4`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `working_capital` is the stock a firm still holds plus what it is owed less what it owes, and `Reporting` publishes it every week off the register and the schedules. `cash_from_operations` is what that tied up against the week's profit, so the two numbers are visibly different                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Firm C4.a`              | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `Reporting` says the week's operating cash and its working capital side by side, and `cash_from_operations` is the profit less what the change in working capital took. A firm whose invoice book and stock grow faster than its margin banks less than it earned, which is the difference the clause is about                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Firm C4.b`              | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `Reporting` sums the ACTUAL rows: stock is the firm's own holdings in packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs, what it is owed is `falling_to` over every unpaid due naming it, and what it owes is its unpaid rows in packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs. No part of it is a share of revenue, and there is no second book to read the stated one against                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Firm D1`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs refuses or queues a payment out of a balance that will not cover it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Firm D2`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `Reporting` reads what a firm owes NOW off its own unpaid rows in packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs, keeping `Owing::Interest` and `Owing::Principal` apart as `Service` — two different things, both of them claims the firm must meet before anything reaches its owners, because the owners' claim is whatever `booked_equity` leaves                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Firm D3`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `coverage` is the week's operating cash over that service and `Reporting` publishes it, so what lenders look at is a read of two numbers the firm actually produced. A firm with nothing owed has NO coverage rather than an infinite one, and the row is simply absent                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Firm D4`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/mortality.rs `trigger_for` now gives an operating firm both doors: `LiabilitiesExceedAssets` where its book is worth less than it owes, and `CouldNotPay` where a due went unmet — so either can happen without the other, and which one did is recorded. Before this a firm could only ever fail the second way, whatever its balance sheet said. Both at once resolves as solvency, as it already does for a bank: the missed payment is the symptom                                                                                                                  |
| `Firm D5`                | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Queue::gave_up` records the arrear when the days run out. Nothing follows: no event, no lender loss, no rating action — the loss chain terminates nowhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Firm E1`                | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `GoodsSellers` posts the price and quantity a firm offers from its own lot basis, its own price outlook and what holding costs it, and `Making` decides how much to make from its own reasons. Which LINES it is in is not a decision: nothing exits a line that neither produces nor sells, so a firm keeps every line it was seeded with                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Firm E2`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` posts a firm's openings from the hours its own expected sales need beyond the hours its engagements already give it, at what an hour is worth to it, and sheds engagements when it has more hours than it wants — so headcount is the firm's own decision from its own state                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Firm E3`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `Building` opens a capital programme only where the firm's own project clears its own cost of capital plus its management's hurdle over its horizon, priced from its own outlook of what it sells and at what, on the ground it stands on — so how much to invest is decided, and a project that does not clear is not built                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Firm E4`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/cost_of_capital.rs publishes what a firm's debt and its equity each cost it at the margin, both derived from what its paper cleared at, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `funds` is the choice: its own cash first, then whichever of borrowing and new shares is cheaper. `Building` makes it when it opens a programme and says which — a borrowing reaches the debt market and an issue reaches packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs `Floating`, which floats what the firm chose. A price move in either market changes the choice |
| `Firm E4.a`              | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `funds` takes whether a firm is above its own leverage target, and the money raised is only ever raised INTO a programme — a firm with none raises nothing. What is absent is the target itself: every firm loan packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs writes is `Unsecured`, so no lender has drawn a firm a leverage line, and no management holds a risk aversion to moderate one. `Building` says there is nothing to be above rather than inventing a line                                                                             |
| `Firm E5`                | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs pays a declared dividend as real cash to the settled holders of record, and only out of what is left once what the firm owes NOW is met — debt service is a claim ahead of the owners, so a firm cannot pay them and then miss its interest. How much of that is paid is still not the firm's own decision: it is one payout ratio applied to every firm's cash result, and no management holds a payout preference of its own. A buyback is seeded as a process and nothing executes one                                                                     |
| `Firm E6`                | PARTIAL | headcount (packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages`), investment (packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `Building`), how to fund it (`funds`) and the price and quantity offered (packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `GoodsSellers`) are each read off the firm's own state and the prices it faces. Two are not: what it pays out is one payout ratio packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs applies to every firm, and which lines it is in is never decided at all                                                                                        |
| `Firm E7`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/expectations.rs `Forming` forms the firm`s own adaptive expectation of what it sells and how much, and `Publishes` publishes a result against it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Firm E7.a`              | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/expectations.rs `Forming` gives every firm its own expectation of what it sells, public or not, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/reporting.rs `Publishes` publishes a result against it. No calendar says when, and no bank publishes an estimate of the same lines                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Firm F1`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs (every leg names both sides; there is no revenue without a buyer and no cost without a payee)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Firm F2`                | MET     | no earnings path exists: a firm`s result is whatever its own legs and marks produced                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Firm F3`                | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs (a firm`s balance is checked before every payment and a short one fails)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Firm F4`                | MISSING | nothing in the tree computes a sector aggregate of firms, so there is no sum to check against its members. The FORBID half holds by that absence — no stored aggregate exists for a firm to be scaled to — but the VERIFY is a measurement, and there is nothing yet to measure                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

| ------------------------ | ------- | -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

| `Capital Programme A4.a` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `Building` names one required plant line for every builder, so what a firm's plant is made of does not vary with its industry. | `Capital Programme A4.b` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `Plant` carries a life, so a good with no life is not plant to anybody. | `Capital Programme A4.c` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs consumes what a firm's own recipe names as an input and packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs treats what carries a life as plant, so two of the three answers are the buyer's. | `Capital Programme A5`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `Building` now names the required plant line and `Builder` bids only for that line; packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs reduces the monetary commitment only after its purchase settles. Funding the commitment and construction after purchase remain open                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Capital Programme A6`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs lots ARE the vintages (units, cost, service date), and `Making` reads capacity over them rather than storing a stock                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Capital Programme A6.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs moves plant only as a `Leg::Asset` with two named sides and records wear as a `Leg::Depreciate` on the holder's own book, so both halves have a shape. | `Capital Programme A6.b` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `PlantMoves` is the one real audit family in the world: it walks the period`s legs, compares them with the register per capital line, and derives its own dust                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Capital Programme B1`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `Building` reads the firm`s own quantity and named-price outlooks, names the required plant line, and `Builder` submits a bounded bid only in that book. | `Capital Programme B1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `Building` reads the firm's own quantity outlook and its own price outlook on what it sells, so what a project is worth comes from what that firm expects to sell and at what level                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Capital Programme B1.b` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/cost_of_capital.rs is where a marginal debt and equity cost would be read, and no builder reads one. | `Capital Programme B1.c` | PARTIAL | `Building` reads price outlooks, and an outlook moves only when a print surprised the firm, so a market price does reach a real investment decision. | `Capital Programme B1.d` | MISSING | no management holds a hurdle or a horizon of its own. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs reads declared numbers from `params` for both, so every firm waits the same time for the same return                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Capital Programme B2`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/capital_programme.rs `Building` now names the required plant line and `Builder` bids only for that line; packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs reduces the monetary commitment only after its purchase settles. | `Trade Credit E2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Queue::joins` asserts the payer is somebody                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Trade Credit E3`   | MISSING | nothing resolves when a debtor ceases — a queued payment owed by a dead party stays queued                                                                                                                                                           |

## Goods

| requirement    | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| -------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Goods A1`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `Class::Good` with its own `UnitId`, and packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs says what one of a unit is divided into                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Goods A2`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `Way`, one row per way of making a line — fixed input quantities per unit of output, plus labour and capital services; packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs is the arithmetic over it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Goods A2.a`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `Way` states a fixed quantity of each input per unit of output and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs consumes exactly that, so a firm short of one input cannot make up the difference with another. Substitution is absent rather than parameterised to zero                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Goods A2.b`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs draws `per x units`, never money divided by a price, so a price doubling does not halve the draw                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Goods A2.c`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `Way` carries the labour hours and the capital services a batch needs beside its material inputs, so all three enter the same recipe and a shortage of any of them is the same kind of constraint                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Goods A2.d`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `Way` carries `extractive`, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `open_to` lets such a way run only where the ground holds a deposit of what it makes. Everything else about it — its labour, its capital services, its yield, its batch and its length — is a way like any other, and packages/kernel-rs/src/bin/world_runs.rs makes two of its goods this way                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Goods A3`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `perishing_of` answers how much of a lot of a line spoils a week — declared once per line — and a line with no answer keeps. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Perishing` reads it per line, so what spoils and how fast is the good's own property rather than one rate applied to every good, dwellings and what comes out of the ground included                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Goods A4`     | MET     | a good is its sub-unit: every unit of one line is the same thing, held as `Lot`s in packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs that differ in what they cost and not in what they are. A thing that differs is a different line with its own id in packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs, which is what the Keys rule says a good is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Goods B1`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` decides from the firm`s own reasons — capacity off its plant vintages, its own outlook of what it sells, the hours its engagements give it and what they cost — and the quantity is what comes out                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Goods B1.a`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` reads capacity off the firm's own plant vintages and cannot start a batch past it, so a firm at capacity is in a state that changes what it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Goods B1.b`   | MET     | `Making` reads the free input units the firm actually holds and starts only the batches they cover, so a shortage stops production rather than being computed and ignored                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Goods B1.c`   | MET     | `Making` reads the hours the firm's own engagements give it, so a firm without the labour for a batch does not start it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Goods B1.d`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` says `utilisation` for each line after it has decided — what the line started against what its plant could serve — so it is a read of the outcome, and nothing reads it back into the decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Goods B2`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` proposes the inputs the line drew as `Leg::Destroy` legs in the same pass (one of the ten propose sites)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Goods B3`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs `InProgress` holds work in progress with what it cost                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Goods B4`     | MET     | a `Way` states what a run yields, packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` finishes only that share of what it started, and `unit_cost` spreads the whole cost of the run over the units that survived — so scrap is a real loss of units where they would have been made, and the survivors are dearer per unit than what was started                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Goods B5`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `unit_cost` is the inputs the run consumed at their own lot basis, plus the wages its hours cost, plus the capital charge its plant services absorbed, over the units that came off the line                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Goods B5.a`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` capitalises nothing into a run that finishes nothing, and what the firm still pays in such a week — its wages and its plant's upkeep — reaches packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `OperatingFlows` as period cost, because no batch absorbed it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Goods B5.b`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `decide` runs a line below what its plant could serve whenever demand, inputs, labour or the ground bind first, and `absorbed` puts the plant's whole week — upkeep and depreciation — on what ran on it, shared by the services each run drew, so `unit_cost` of a line run at half its rate carries twice the capital per unit                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Goods C1`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `GoodsSellers` offers the quantities a maker holds at a level from its own cost and outlook, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/households.rs `HouseholdBuyers` and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Stockist` post the most they will pay — both sides meet in packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Goods C2`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs and packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs: a goods book prints per (market, instrument, period) with the market naming a place, and the print is stored so the next period can re-mark against it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Goods C3`     | PARTIAL | buyers bid for their own reasons: packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `GoodsSellers` has a maker BUY its inputs — what its expected runs take beyond what it holds, at no more than `GoodIsWorthWhatItMakes` says the input is worth to it — households buy to consume, a stockist buys to hold, and an estate sells through the forced-sale participant. Government procurement and foreign buyers are absent                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Goods C4`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `clear` rations by the rule the book states — `Rationing::ProRata` for the goods venues — so when demand exceeds supply the rule is written once, in the solver, and not by the market                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Goods C5`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs (what did not fill rests or lapses; unsold output stays on the seller`s row)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Goods C6`     | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/assembly.rs `open_book` prices a book `Placed::In` a region in the money of its region's country, read through the registry, so a good is priced where its seller stands and in the seller's currency, and each side pays out of its own account in it. No buyer from another currency exists to buy the seller's money first: the world has one currency, which is Cross-Border's (block 7)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Goods D1`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs dispatches goods sold to a buyer in another place aboard a vehicle standing where they are, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Arrives` lands them at the destination — they move physically, and nothing teleports                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Goods D2`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `arrives_in` takes the route's length over the vehicle's speed plus loading, so moving takes time, and packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Leg::Dispatch` consumes the carriage the owner paid a carrier for, so it costs money                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Goods D3`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Sells` is a carrier offering room on its own vehicles, and the carriage book pays it for every unit of room a shipper buys — the freight is earned by a named party                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Goods D4`     | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Leg::Dispatch` consumes the room the owner bought and capitalises what it cost onto the parcel that arrived, over the price the same instruction settled — so a landed lot's basis is ex-works plus freight, written by settlement and read from packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs rather than recomputed. Duty is absent: no authority levies one, and absent is not a duty of nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Goods D5`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Dispatch` names one owner for goods in transit, and `CargoHasAnOwner` refuses cargo whose owner is not live — so what is between two places is on somebody's book the whole way                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Goods E1`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs (a holding is lots, each carrying what it cost)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Goods E2`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `carry` is lower of cost and net realisable value with the broker-dealer exception, and `Marking` runs it at g1 over every lot of a `Class::Good` a holder carries at cost, against what that line's own book last printed. A line nobody has priced is not priced, and its stock stays at what it cost                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Goods E2.a`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Marking` proposes a `Leg::Depreciate`, which only ever reduces a lot's basis — so a write-down cannot be reversed and an unrealised gain on ordinary inventory reaches nothing. `carry` returns nothing to income at or above cost                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Goods E2.b`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Stockist` declares `Carrying::Market`, so `booked_equity` reads its stock at `carrying_value`, the price its book prints, and the move in that price moves its equity. A line nobody has priced leaves its equity not read                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Goods E2.c`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Marking` skips any holding not carried at `Carrying::Cost` and books only a charge, never a credit, so a non-broker-dealer's warehouse cannot be marked up when the market rises.
