| `Goods E3`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Marking` says `goods.written_down` with the line and the charge in the week it happens, beside the leg that reduces the basis — so the holding loss is an event with a date, a size and a holder rather than a number that moved                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Goods E4`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Perishing` (a `Leg::Destroy` with `Gone::Perished` removes units at the lot`s own cost and the wire records the disposal)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Goods E4.a`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Perishing` destroys units with `Gone::Perished` and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/commodities.rs `Storing` proposes a cash fee to the stockist, so the two are different things in different places. | `Goods F1`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs settles every fill as a `Leg::Money` from the buyer's own account to the named seller in the same instruction as the `Leg::Asset` that delivers the goods, so payment is by name and against delivery                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Goods F2`     | MET     | payment is immediate by default, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/trade_credit.rs `TradeCredit` gives a buyer who is short the seller's agreement to wait until a stated date — so terms are either immediate or trade credit with a due date                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Goods F3`     | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/trade_credit.rs lets a seller wait, and nothing makes the wait a loan that can go bad: no receivable is written off and no seller takes a loss when a buyer it waited for fails. That is the Trade Credit block's (block 4)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Goods F4`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `OffersItsRoom` sells room on a route as an instrument, and the carriage book's fill pays the carrier for it — the freight goes to the carrier by name, before the goods move                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Goods F5`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs says a disposal's proceeds and what the lots that left cost, in the one pass that holds the price and the basis at once, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `OperatingFlows::sold` takes both — so revenue is recognised when the units are delivered and the charge against it is the cost of those units, valued per E5 by the lots `debit` drew                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Goods F5.a`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/firms.rs `OperatingFlows::reads` no longer treats money paid for an input as a cost — it is stock the firm now holds — and charges only a wage, which no batch absorbed. So a firm that produces and does not sell carries the cost in its stock, and an idle line's payroll is what makes idle capacity expensive                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Goods F5.b`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs (a batch`s cost is capitalised into the lot and is not also a period expense — one cost, one place)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Goods G1`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/benchmarks.rs `PublishedIndices` publishes every declared index every week at g3, the consumer and producer baskets among them, each from the constituents and weights the registry states and each only when every constituent of it priced. | `Goods G2`     | MISSING | no mechanism reads inflation at all, so no index is ever named as the one a change was measured on                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| `Goods G4`     | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` says each line's `utilisation` after it has decided how much to start, as a read of what it started against what its plant could serve                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| requirement    | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

| `Freight A1`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Sells` makes each carrier's week of room on every route it serves out of the plant it holds, at what that plant costs it, and destroys what nobody bought — so capacity is per route, fixed in the week, and perishable. `OffersItsRoom` posts it into that route's own book at the basis the room carries. `Ships` is the other side: a party with goods to move bids what not being able to deliver would cost it — its best margin on what it holds — for as much as its own outlook says it will sell, on the routes that start where it stands. Both sides meet in `clearing::clear` like every other book, and the fill's own money leg is what pays the carrier. Nothing has cleared yet because no goods line has printed, so no shipper can price its margin |

| `Freight B2.a` | MISSING | no freight price clears, so nothing can be inelastic or move violently. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Sells` makes a fixed week of room and destroys what nobody bought, which is the supply side of what would make it move — and no shipper bids against it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Freight B3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `running_of` is what moving one unit costs THIS vehicle, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `OffersItsRoom` posts each vehicle's lot at its own — so a carrier with a cheap ship and a dear one offers at two levels, which is a supply schedule rather than one number repeated                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Freight B4`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Sells` makes each carrier's week of room on every route it serves out of the plant it holds, at what that plant costs it, and destroys what nobody bought — so capacity is per route, fixed in the week, and perishable. `OffersItsRoom` posts it into that route's own book at the basis the room carries. `Ships` is the other side: a party with goods to move bids what not being able to deliver would cost it — its best margin on what it holds — for as much as its own outlook says it will sell, on the routes that start where it stands. Both sides meet in `clearing::clear` like every other book, and the fill's own money leg is what pays the carrier. Nothing has cleared yet because no goods line has printed, so no shipper can price its margin |
| `Freight B5`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/geography.rs `Vehicle` is an individual thing on a TILE — a position and the line it is, and nothing else — and `vehicles_at` is what packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Sells` asks at each route's origin — so a carrier's room on a route is what is standing there and not what its owner happens to hold somewhere else                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Freight B5.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/geography.rs `Vehicle` names the LINE it is, so who owns it is the register's answer and a sale is an ordinary transfer of that unit — after which it is the same ship. | `Freight B5.b` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Arrives` leaves the vehicle at the site it delivered to and packages/kernel-rs/src/geography.rs `moves_to` writes it, so where it ends the week is where it starts the next — and a carrier whose ships are all at the far end of their last run has nothing standing where the next cargo is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Freight B6`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Dispatch` names the VEHICLE its cargo is aboard, `used` is that vehicle's room and not its carrier's, and `arrived` decides what became of the shipment from what became of the carrier — so a cargo shares the fate of the thing carrying it rather than being a quantity that cannot have one                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Freight B7`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `running_of` is what a voyage burns and `Plant::upkeep_per_period` is what the week costs whether it sails or not — two declared numbers, neither derived from the other. `Sells` makes each vehicle's room at its RUNNING cost, so an idle vehicle is not as expensive as a working one                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Freight B8`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Sells` walks the vehicles standing at a route's origin and makes each one's room as its own lot at its own cost — nothing is divided by the number of routes, and a vehicle standing at one origin makes room on no other                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Freight C1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Ships` bids only where it holds goods with a margin on them, so the demand exists because somebody is trading and not on its own account                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Freight C1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Ships` sizes its booking by its own `HOW_MUCH_IT_SELLS` outlook against what it actually holds, so what it books moves with what it expects to trade                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `Freight C2`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/freight.rs `Sells` makes each carrier's week of room on every route it serves out of the plant it holds, at what that plant costs it, and destroys what nobody bought — so capacity is per route, fixed in the week, and perishable. `OffersItsRoom` posts it into that route's own book at the basis the room carries. `Ships` is the other side: a party with goods to move bids what not being able to deliver would cost it — its best margin on what it holds — for as much as its own outlook says it will sell, on the routes that start where it stands. Both sides meet in `clearing::clear` like every other book, and the fill's own money leg is what pays the carrier. Nothing has cleared yet because no goods line has printed, so no shipper can price its margin |

## Labour

| requirement   | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Labour A1`   | MET     | `agreed::ENGAGEMENT` is hours of a named household`s time supplied to a named firm, with terms `[wage per person per period, hours per person per period, headcount]`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Labour A2`   | MET     | see A1 — the wage is per unit of time, in the employer`s money                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Labour A3`   | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` clears ONE book over every firm and every household, so a job in one occupation is a job in all of them. Skill, sector and region divide nothing, and there is no key on which a posting and a seeker could fail to meet                                                                                                                                          |
| `Labour A3.a` | MISSING | there is one labour book and no skill or place divides it, so a seeker and a vacancy that cannot meet do not exist. Unemployment and vacancies cannot be high together because the market has no segments to be mismatched across                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Labour A3.b` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `moving` and `enters_at` are the flow of people and the entry wage, and nothing calls either: with one book there is nowhere for supply to move to                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `Labour A4`   | MET     | `agreed::ENGAGEMENT` persists across periods and is a state rather than a per-period trade                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Labour A4.a` | PARTIAL | `agreed::ENGAGEMENT` names a firm, a worker and a wage and persists across weeks, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` pays off it, hires into it and separates out of it. One read is still absent: `unemployment` has no caller, so no rate is derived from the state                                                                                                                            |
| `Labour A4.b` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs hires in WHOLE people — the book's fills are integer counts and `continuing_engagements` writes them as an engagement headcount — and asserts the headcount never exceeds the cell's weight. A cohort is a named party the engagement can tell                                                                                                                            |
| `Labour A4.c` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` splits a cell whose engagement covers only part of it, carrying the engagement to the members who took the job, and `can_end` refuses to separate an engagement that is not the whole cell — so a cell is never part employed at the end of a week                                                                                                                |
| `Labour B1`   | MISSING | every unemployed household offers its whole weight at whatever the book gives. There is no participation decision, so no household weighs the wage against its alternatives and none stays out                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Labour B1.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `outside_option` reads what a household is actually paid for not working off its own transfer agreements, and a seeker posts its offer at that floor — it takes nothing below it, and it is also a floor under staying. Nothing pays a benefit yet, so every seeker posts unpriced and the floor is absent rather than nothing                                            |
| `Labour B2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `offers` posts a cell's own `weight` and nothing more, so total employment is capped by the stock of living people and by nothing else. There is no workforce number anywhere                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Labour B3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs `household_employment` carries employed or unemployed as a coordinate on the household lattice, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs moves between them only by `splits` or `transitions` — a population event with a dated journal row                                                                                                                                  |
| `Labour B4`   | MISSING | a seeker the book left over is a seeker again next week and nothing else happened to it. Search takes no time, so unemployment is whatever the week's book did not reach rather than a state anybody is in                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Labour B5`   | MISSING | no audit contribution reconciles employed plus unemployed plus inactive against the population. The lattice makes the sum checkable and nothing checks it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Labour C1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` bids what an hour is worth to the firm — its own outlook price for what it makes, divided by the labour that unit needs — for the hours its expected sales need beyond the ones it already has. Below that it does not post                                                                                                                                       |
| `Labour C1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` reads the firm's own `price_of` outlook and counts only outputs whose plant `ctx.register()` says it holds — so its capital decides what it can want an hour for and its own price decides what the hour is worth                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Labour C2`   | MISSING | a fill this week is an engagement this week, producing at once. Finding costs nothing and there is no time before the person is productive                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Labour C3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Separation::owed` charges the contractual wage weeks on a dismissal and nothing on a quit, and `Wages` owes it as a due on the employer's account — so shedding is dearer than holding and the asymmetry is in the contract, not in a pair of speeds                                                                                                                     |
| `Labour C4`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` ends an engagement whose employer is no longer alive as `Ended::EmployerGone`, down the same separation path as a dismissal: the worker goes to unemployed and the severance is owed, which ranks in the estate like any other claim                                                                                                                              |
| `Labour C5`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Posting` is a real intention owned by the employer that bid it, and it goes unfilled when the book does not reach its level. It lives one week and is re-posted, so there is nothing standing to withdraw                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Labour D1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` posts every employer's opening as a bid at the wage it offers and every seeker's heads as supply, and packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `clear` — the one solver — strikes it with `BuyersCompete` and `Rationing::Priority`. The highest bids take their places whole, a tie shares pro rata, and the level that took the last place is the price and the print |
| `Labour D1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `by_level` fills the keener level whole before the next gets anything, so an employer that bids above the going rate fills more than one that bids below it. A single ratio over everyone inside the price is the other rule, and the labour book does not use it                                                                                                                      |
| `Labour D1.b` | MISSING | a firm that was rationed and a firm that filled bid the same thing next week. Nothing carries the week's fill into an outlook, so no gap is closed at anybody's horizon                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Labour D1.c` | MISSING | nothing reads a going rate off what is actually paid. The engagements carry the wages that would be weighted and no mechanism walks them for an average                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Labour D2`   | PARTIAL | a standing engagement keeps the wage it was struck at: packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs pays the agreement's own term and nothing rewrites it, so the week's print does not reach anybody already employed. There is no renegotiation, so there is no renegotiation cost either — stickiness is total rather than costly                                                                                   |
| `Labour D2.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` posts places when it is short of hours and pushes `Ended::Dismissed` when it has a surplus, and neither touches a standing `AgreementTerms::Engagement` wage. The whole adjustment is in the headcount                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Labour D2.b` | MET     | there is no coefficient damping a wage anywhere in packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs. A standing wage does not move because `AgreementTerms::Engagement` is a term nothing rewrites, which is the contract doing it                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Labour D3`   | MISSING | one book, and everybody in it meets everybody. Every unemployed person is reachable by every vacancy, so nothing is imperfect about the matching                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Labour D4`   | MISSING | neither unemployment nor vacancies is measured, so they cannot be seen moving against each other. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `unemployment` is the read and has no caller                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Labour D5`   | MET     | the only route from a price to a wage is `Wages`'s own bid in packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs, its output price outlook per labour hour. There is no cost-of-living adjustment and no second channel to the going rate                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Labour E1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` proposes the payment: the week`s wages leave the employer`s account and reach the worker (one of the ten propose sites)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `Labour E2`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs carries wages into batch unit cost and GoodsSellers combines own price outlook, lot basis and holding cost into its ask; broader labour-price feedback remains incomplete                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Labour E2.a` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs carries wages into the unit cost of a batch, so a wage is a cost that reaches a price. It is not demand: no household spends a wage it received, because no wage changes, so which side dominates is not a question the world can answer                                                                                                                                       |
| `Labour E3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `tax_payers` levies income tax on the worker and payroll tax on the employer off every settled `Receipt::Wage` leg, so the wage is taxed where it lands and by named payers                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Labour E4`   | MISSING | a separation moves the cell to unemployed and nothing in credit reads that coordinate, so losing a job changes no household's ability to service its debt                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Labour F1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` pays out of the named employer`s own account; there is no employment that is not an engagement between two named parties                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Labour F2`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/employment.rs `Wages` asserts `heads > 0 && heads <= weight`, so a wage bill without headcount and a headcount above the workforce are both refused at the site                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Labour F3`   | MET     | no unemployment rate exists anywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

## Housing

| requirement       | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Housing A1`      | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `Class::Plant` with a unit, held by a named party, and packages/kernel-rs/src/places.rs `is_a_structure` puts it in a region. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs moves exactly one unit at a time, so a dwelling is counted and never divided. Its unit is a structure line rather than a dwelling of its own, so two houses on one line are two units and not two addresses |
| `Housing A1.a`    | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/places.rs puts every dwelling in a region and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `clearing` crosses bids against offers per place, so location is part of what a dwelling is and there is a market per place. Nothing settles in any of them, so the several markets produce no several prices                                                                                              |
| `Housing A2`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs originates the rent a tenancy owes as a `Owing::Rent` due, so the shelter is consumed by a named occupier and paid for; the same dwelling is a holding in the register carrying what it cost. Both are present at once, on the same unit                                                                                                                                        |
| `Housing A3`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs strikes an `agreed::TENANCY` between the owner of a spare roof and a household that has none, and originates the rent under it every week. The landlord is the holder of the dwelling in packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs — there is no rental stock without dwellings behind it                                                                                              |
| `Housing A4`      | PARTIAL | the stock is packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs's holdings of structure lines, moved only by what changes hands: packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs proposes a `Leg::Asset` of one dwelling per sale and one per foreclosure. Nothing is BUILT, so the stock can only move between owners and a completion never adds to it                                                                                        |
| `Housing A5`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `Housing` originates each owner's upkeep as an invoice from a named local supplier, per dwelling the register says it actually holds — so maintenance is a real cost that reaches somebody's account rather than value leaving a balance sheet                                                                                                                                  |
| `Housing B1`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `clearing` crosses bids against offers per location and `Housing` runs one book per place that has an offer, proposing a `Leg::Asset` against purchase money for each cross                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Housing B1.a`    | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `offered` gives an offer a REASON: what an owner holds beyond the one roof it lives under, and everything held by a party that lives nowhere. `Offer::reserving` takes the greater of what the seller owes and what a dwelling costs to build there. The other source is absent: nothing is built, so no builder's completion reaches the book                                  |
| `Housing B1.b`    | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `keenest_for` gives a buyer the most generous standard among the lenders standing WHERE IT IS, and `can_bid` turns that into what it can borrow. A buyer no lender quotes to bids its own money and no more                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Housing B1.c`    | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `clearing` pairs the keenest bid with the cheapest reservation and stops at the first pair that does not meet, so an offer no bid reaches stays unsold and prints nothing                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Housing B2`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs builds every bid from `can_bid` — the lending standard's income multiple and deposit share against the buyer's own income and deposit — so what it can borrow, not what it wants, is what it offers                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Housing B2.a`    | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `can_bid` reads the income multiple and deposit share of the standard the buyer faces, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_capital.rs `standard` writes that standing from the lender's own position. A tightening therefore reaches the next price directly                                                                                                             |
| `Housing B3`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `offered` keeps an owner's own home off the book, so the week's supply is what somebody has a reason to sell and not the whole stock. A shift in what buyers can borrow moves the price against that supply rather than calling more of it out                                                                                                                                  |
| `Housing B4`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `Offer::reserving` is a refusal: a seller does not take less than it owes or less than a dwelling costs to build, and `clearing` leaves it unsold. With `offered` keeping homes off the book, a market where bids fall below reservations simply stops trading                                                                                                                  |
| `Housing B4.a`    | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `sample` is the share of what was offered that actually changed hands, and `Housing` says it beside every print — so a reader of the price is told the sample it came from rather than left to treat a changing one as fixed                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Housing B5`      | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `yield_on` is the read and has no caller. Rents and prices both exist, and nothing puts one against the other or against another yield                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Housing C1`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs brings a `loan_claim` from the named lender secured on the dwelling it financed — a transferable row in packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs like any other loan, with the house pledged against it                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Housing C2`      | PARTIAL | the `loan_claim` packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs brings carries a tenor and a loan-to-value covenant, and the kernel's own schedule falls due against it. Whether the rate is fixed or floating is not a term anybody wrote, so no mortgage resets                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Housing C3`      | MISSING | nothing reads a loan against the current price of the house behind it. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_capital.rs `standard` takes the inverse of a capital ratio where the clause wants the loan-to-value cross-section of the lender's own book                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `Housing C4`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `foreclose` runs on a breached `agreed::MORTGAGE`: it proposes the `Leg::Asset` that moves the dwelling from borrower to lender, opens a workout on that exact title, and returns the house to the book as the lender's own offer                                                                                                                                               |
| `Housing C4.a`    | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs pushes `foreclose`'s `returns_as` into the SAME week's offers, at what the lender must fetch — so the seized supply is in the book that clears next, which is what makes a falling price fall further. Nothing reduces a principal in place                                                                                                                                     |
| `Housing C4.b`    | MISSING | a foreclosure needs a breached mortgage and nothing marks one breached, so none has happened and none has coincided with another. The seizure and the return to the book are built; what is missing is the default that starts them                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Housing C5`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_capital.rs `standard` is a decision each lender takes from its own position and writes as `standing::LENDING_STANDARD`, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs reads only what a lender published. It moves week to week; it is not a constant                                                                                                                              |
| `Housing C5.a`    | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/bank_capital.rs `standard` reads the lender's own headroom and hurdle, so two lenders with different books quote differently. It stands in for the loan-to-value cross-section with the inverse of the capital ratio, which is a shape and not the measurement the clause names                                                                                                            |
| `Housing C5.b`    | MET     | the standard moves with the lender's own book, `keenest_for` puts it in front of the buyers that can reach it, and the price those bids clear is what the next dwelling sells at — so tightening reaches the price and the price reaches the next book. The loop closes                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Housing C6`      | MISSING | a mortgage is a `loan_claim` and therefore transferable, and nothing pools or sells one. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/securitisation.rs takes no mortgage claim                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Housing D1`      | MISSING | a house price change is a change in what a holder's units are worth and nothing reads it as wealth. No household's spending moves because the dwelling it holds is worth more                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `Housing D2`      | MISSING | nothing builds a dwelling, so housing construction is neither investment nor employment. `Housing` only moves houses that already stand                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Housing D3`      | MISSING | rent is owed and paid as `Owing::Rent`, and no consumer price level exists for it to be a component of                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Housing D4`      | PARTIAL | the `loan_claim` packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs brings is a real liability of the household and its service falls due on the kernel's schedule, so it is a fixed claim on income. Nothing compares it with the household's other liabilities, so whether it is the largest is not asked                                                                                                                        |
| `Housing D5`      | MISSING | no rate rise reaches consumption by either route, because no household's spending reads its debt service or what its dwelling is worth                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `Housing E1`      | MET     | a dwelling is a holding in packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs and nothing else, so `holder_of` always answers and no owner can hold one it is not on the register for. The register's own `held_total` refuses units held against nothing issued                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Housing E2`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs `clearing` crosses real bids against real offers; no price path exists                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `Housing E3`      | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/housing.rs brings every mortgage as a `loan_claim` held by the named lender, so it is an asset on that lender's balance sheet from the week it is written. There is no mortgage anybody holds nothing against                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Housing E4`      | MET     | a mortgage has ONE representation — the `loan_claim` row — so what households owe and what lenders hold are the two directions of the same register rows, and packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs's own `held_total` family is what checks they agree. A second reconciliation would need a second book to reconcile against                                                                                                  |

## Cross-Border

| requirement         | status  | where / why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Cross-Border A1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs `region_of`, read by packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/cross_border.rs `CrossBorder` to decide a leg crossed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `Cross-Border A2`   | MISSING | the world has one currency and, per 12.1, `households`, `insurers`, `money_market`, `freight` and `dealing` are participants without their mechanisms — so there is no foreign asset, no foreign-currency borrower and no bank funding in one money and lending in another. `cross_border.rs Exposure`, `Finances`, `currency_gap`, `default_reaches`, `exports_to`, `financed_by` and `in_buyers_money` are unreached |
| `Cross-Border A2.a` | MISSING | the world has one currency and, per 12.1, `households`, `insurers`, `money_market`, `freight` and `dealing` are participants without their mechanisms — so there is no foreign asset, no foreign-currency borrower and no bank funding in one money and lending in another. `cross_border.rs Exposure`, `Finances`, `currency_gap`, `default_reaches`, `exports_to`, `financed_by` and `in_buyers_money` are unreached |
| `Cross-Border A3`   | MISSING | a payment does NOT reach an account in the currency it was made in — `across` lands it in the payee`s bank`s money whatever that money is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Cross-Border A4`   | MISSING | the world has one currency and, per 12.1, `households`, `insurers`, `money_market`, `freight` and `dealing` are participants without their mechanisms — so there is no foreign asset, no foreign-currency borrower and no bank funding in one money and lending in another. `cross_border.rs Exposure`, `Finances`, `currency_gap`, `default_reaches`, `exports_to`, `financed_by` and `in_buyers_money` are unreached |

| ---------------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Reporting A1`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/reporting.rs `Publishes` publishes a result per firm on a calendar placed by the day the company started, and A1.a IS read: it walks every living party, sums what outsiders hold of each share line off packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs, and asks `reporting::reports(listed, outsiders)`. There is no kind of party that reports (Law 15). The row said the opposite until 0j.7 read the source                                                                            |
| `Reporting A1.a` | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/reporting.rs `Publishes::run` derives public status each period from a listed share line and outsider holdings in the register; no reporting party kind is consulted                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Reporting A2`   | PARTIAL | `Publishes` reads the equity account and the journal, so G2`s "the equity account`s movement over the fiscal period" is read off the record the events moved. What it reads is the account as it stands today rather than as it stood at the fiscal close, because a date-week has no inverse on the one calendar                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `Reporting A2.a` | MET     | `Publishes` composes nothing: every figure is a read of the register and the journal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `Reporting A3`   | MET     | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/reporting.rs `Fiscal` carries the quarter a report is for — when it opened, when it closed and when it is published — and each company's runs from the week it started rather than from a shared calendar. It is placed by date: the production path advances the company's own start date by three months at a time and closes the books on the tick before the one that date falls on                                                                                         |

| `Observer A1`   | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs `Print` carries its instrument, period, unit and provenance, so a stale mark is visibly stale (A1.a).
